- **JS6 The pick and the Switch (N-17, N-19, N-28, N-31, G2-G4).** An explicit pick acts on the set's own untouched Normal slot. A PBR-family pick writes `pbr_normal`. A regular pick restores the engine suggestion (`landscape_normal` when the path has a `landscape` folder, else `normal`) except when R5 supplies Eye's `msn`. Eye writes `msn` once and parks the listed tangent word. When R5 returns that free `msn` to a tangent type, the new shader decides the word and `auto_normal` lists it. Auto clears the Eye type and its listing (N6-c, N6-d). Auto from an explicit PBR-family pick clears an untouched `pbr_normal`. Both Auto and the diffuse's Revert read the current pick from the sets document, even before the plan lands (N6-g, N6-h). The key leaves `auto_pbr`. Other stored tangent types are the user's, except for the leave step clearing a key an older pick listed in `auto_pbr`. The diffuse's Switch to `pbr_albedo` or `skin_albedo` can retype any free inferred `normal` or `landscape_normal`, or an untouched listed tangent member. An authored alpha policy protects even an inferred normal. Listed members stay in `auto_normal` with the new word, inferred ones join `auto_pbr`, and a model-space member keeps `msn` (IA3). Revert keeps an untouched `pbr_normal` under an explicit PBR-family pick, including Advanced Skin. Otherwise it restores the engine type and the recorder may follow once more (N-31). A shader pick clears the follow marker only when it moves the key away from `pbr_normal`. This closes JQ54's leftover for the Normal slot.

## Note HM — UI-proof workflow: headless driver, frame checks, journeys (main team, 2026-11-07)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW1, 2026-11-20)
- **HM5 Frame checks (crates/gui/tests/ui_drive/checks.rs).** Seven mechanical checks run on every frame and are written as `NN.checks.json` (run.json `findings` counts them per check, a check step's note is a `filter` finding with rule `oracle <name>`); each finding carries its rule text: `names` (egui-ui-rules 8.2), `clipped_text` (3.4), `off_window` (3.1/3.3), `overlap` (3.1), `alignment` and `lonely_wrap` (owner 2026-11-07 design, Why), `painted_only` (8.2 and design §2). Siblings are nodes with the same parent and the same size (within 1 px, at least 8x8), not the same role: the set card's empty chips are `Label` nodes and its filled ones `Button` nodes. `alignment` accepts a matching top-left or centre offset, so left-aligned names of different widths pass; a sibling with no painted text is flagged only when most siblings have some; one row whose tops differ by more than 1 px is flagged too. `painted_only` matches whole words, skips glyph-only text and checks each piece of a middle-elided text separately; the naming node has to touch the text's rect, not hold its centre, because the rect of rotated or right-aligned text is wider than the glyphs. `clipped_text` and `off_window` list only left and right cuts (rule 3.4 is about truncation; a row half scrolled out at the top or bottom is not a defect); the rest are counted in one `summary` finding per frame, with the number of Labels whose clip is unknown (egui's widget rect and the AccessKit rect disagree by 1 px or more, as in a scrolled ScrollArea; those are checked against the window only). `names` skips thin drag-only handles (8 px or thinner, no role) and scroll bars; `overlap` skips scroll bars. Clipping comes from egui's interact_rect and hover text is not probed (a hover would change the replay). On `demo` with an empty script (frame 00), 1055x770: alignment 7, names 16, overlap 7, summary 1; at the default 1600x900: alignment 8, names 20, overlap 10, painted_only 0, summary 1. The Sets-column finding names the empty slot chips G, CNR and S (centred letter) against the filled chips (bottom-left letter); the other findings are the Textures tiles (unnamed, 3 px vertical overlap, no letter on some).

## Note HN — Sets column polish: docked helpers and the first UI-proof findings (main team, 2026-11-07)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-19)
- **HN6 Card titles are elided in the middle and keep the set's unique tail (F3; the pattern of CD4; review E24, JR5).** The title is shortened to what the column's width holds, measured on the laid-out text (`sets_column::fit_title`): the whole id, then the middle elided (`header::elide_middle`) where the result still ends with the set's unique tail (`SetView.short`, JR2), then `…/<tail>`. It is never cut inside the tail. The title shares a row with the lead and the status pill only when the shown title fits there; otherwise it takes its own line at the card's full width, beside the position and handle, with the pill below it, and it wraps only when `…/<tail>` does not fit on that line either. While a search is on and its folded text lies inside one middle segment, the title tries `…/<that segment>/<rest>` right after the whole id (HP7). The full id is the label's AccessKit name and, when shortened, its only hover line.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **HN2 The empty-chip reading (Decision 2).** The "empty target chip" of the spec is a packed output with no source file of its own, such as RMAOS built from helpers only. The planned output is a member of the set, so that chip is an `Own` chip and takes its badges. A `SlotFill::Empty` chip (the slot has no file at all) draws only its letter, bottom-left and dimmed (HP9), and takes no badge.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **HN6 Card titles are elided in the middle (F3; the pattern of CD4).** The title is shortened to what the column's width holds, measured on the laid-out text: the whole id, then the middle elided (`header::elide_middle`: first segment and leaf), then `…/<leaf>`, then the end of the leaf. When the leaf does not fit beside the position and handle, the title takes its own line. While a search is on and its folded text lies inside one middle segment (not the first, not the leaf), the title tries `…/<that segment>/<rest>` right after the whole id (HP7). The full id is the label's AccessKit name and, when shortened, its only hover line.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW7, 2026-11-20)
- **HN1 Helpers are docked on the chips they fill (Decisions 3-5; crates/gui/src/ui/sets_column.rs).** A helper is drawn as a 16 px badge (`BADGE`) centred on the top-right corner of each slot chip it fills, half outside the chip (HP1), on a filled chip and on a packed output chip (HN2), with a 1 px panel-colour outline and a 2 px stroke while selected; chips sit `DOCK_GAP_X` 16 px apart across and `DOCK_GAP_Y` 8 px down, `DOCK_INSET` 2 px in from the card edge, so the badge lies in the gap. The hit rect starts at 20 px (`BADGE_HIT`) and `clamp_hits` cuts it to the card and to the shared edge of any other badge, chip, tile or combo (never under 18 px at 1055x770 and 1400x790). A second helper takes the top-left corner, and K helpers that do not fit fold into one `+K` cell (hover lists them). A padlock (kept by PGPatcher, locked pin) keeps its corner and the badge uses the next free one. A badge is a Button named "<helper file>, fills <slot> <channel>" (HP2: `fill_text`, "RMAOS R"); hover shows the file and the helper panel's fills line; click selects the helper (opens the helper panel); right-click is the member menu. A chip with docked helpers is named "<letter>, <item>, filled by <helper files>"; a chip without helpers keeps its bare letter. Only a helper that got a badge leaves the trailing row; one folded into `+K` or with no free cell stays there as well. This amends CD8 ("Chips are named by their letter") for chips with helpers; CD8's plain `_m` thumbnail look is kept.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW1, 2026-11-20)
- **HN2 The empty-chip reading (Decision 2).** The "empty target chip" of the spec is a packed output with no source file of its own, such as RMAOS built from helpers only. The planned output is a member of the set, so that chip is an `Own` chip and takes its badges. An empty slot's chip (the slot has no file at all, `SlotShown::Empty`; a link is not empty, JR1) draws only its letter, bottom-left and dimmed (HP9), and takes no badge.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW7, 2026-11-20)
- **HN3 The trailing row holds what no chip shows, and names each member (M1).** It holds a helper that fills nothing (dimmed by the disabled alpha, name and hover "unused: …", HP12), a helper whose target has no chip on the card, a `_m` env mask and a clashing second member of a kind. A helper reads "<file>, <fills text>" (the HP2 wording), any other member "<file>, <role>", also in its hover. Each tile also paints a short tag bottom-left (HP9): its slot letter when the slot is taken, "unused" for an unused helper, the first fill ("M G") for a helper whose fills land on no chip, otherwise the member's tail ("_m", or "—" when empty); a tag wider than the tile is cut with `…`. This replaces "Members in no chip follow as plain thumbnails" with a row that says why each member is there.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW7, 2026-11-20)
- **HN1 Helpers are docked on the chips they fill (Decisions 3-5; crates/gui/src/ui/sets_column.rs).** A helper is drawn as a 16 px badge (`BADGE`) centred on the top-right corner of each slot chip it fills, half outside the chip (HP1), on a filled chip and on a packed output chip (HN2), with a 1 px panel-colour outline and a 2 px stroke while selected; chips sit `DOCK_GAP_X` 16 px apart across and `DOCK_GAP_Y` 8 px down, `DOCK_INSET` 2 px in from the card edge, so the badge lies in the gap. The hit rect starts at 20 px (`BADGE_HIT`) and `clamp_hits` cuts it to the card and to the shared edge of any other badge, chip, tile or combo (never under 18 px at 1055x770 and 1400x790). A second helper takes the top-left corner when available. When the fills exceed the free corners, the last free cell is a `+K` button listing the distinct folded helpers. One free corner can hold just `+K`; with no free corners or on a linked chip, there is no dock (Note JW7). A padlock (kept by PGPatcher, locked pin) keeps its corner and the badge uses the next free one. A badge is a Button named "<helper file>, fills <slot> <channel>" (HP2: `fill_text`, "RMAOS R"); hover shows the file and the helper panel's fills line; click selects the helper (opens the helper panel); right-click is the member menu. A chip with docked helpers is named "<letter>, <item>, filled by <helper files>"; a chip without helpers keeps its bare letter. A helper leaves the trailing row when a visible badge or an accessible `+K` list represents it. This amends CD8 ("Chips are named by their letter") for chips with helpers; CD8's plain `_m` thumbnail look is kept.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW7, 2026-11-20)
- **HN3 The trailing row holds what no chip shows, and names each member (M1).** It holds a helper that fills nothing (dimmed by the disabled alpha, name and hover "unused: …", HP12), a helper whose target has no chip on the card, a `_m` env mask and a clashing second member of a kind. A helper reads "<file>, <fills text>" (the HP2 wording), any other member "<file>, <role>", also in its hover. A helper gets a badge-size thumbnail with no painted tag. Its trailing tile is suppressed only when a visible badge or accessible overflow list represents it (Note JW7). Other members paint their slot letter when the slot is taken, otherwise their member tail, cut with `…` if needed (HP9).

## Note JW — set cards and dragging (main team, 2026-11-20)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW4, 2026-11-20)
- **JW4 Dragging a shared chip (A6).** Only a displayed `SlotShown::Borrowed` starts `DragBorrow { item, from }`. A drop shares that file directly into the receiving card or strip owner’s document id, including a keyless card (JV1). Each card zone takes the payload type in flight, since egui 0.37 consumes the payload before checking its type. `member_acts` handles `remove` separately from `ChipDrag`: only own members offer “Remove from set”. Explicit linked chips have no drag payload. During a share the new-set zone draws a plain frame so egui cannot highlight it. Releasing there does nothing. Supersedes the CD8 and K13 no-drag rule and amends DU5.
  - On the source set the status is “Nothing to share: {name} is already in {set}.” For a displayed borrow matching both item and owner, directly or through a chain (JN1), it is “{set} already shares {name} from {from}.” Neither adds an undo step.
  - An explicit link effective in the displayed or resulting view gives “{set}'s {slot} is linked to {target}: Unlink it first.” An effective clear gives “{set}'s {slot} is cleared: use Use this file or Unlink first.” Both leave the command, both documents and Undo intact (SC-1). A Share that would remove the destination’s last owned texture is refused on release, before Replace (SC-11).
  - An occupied local, borrowed or record slot otherwise asks Replace. Release and confirmation validate current document membership, the lender and the exact numbered-slot item, using the same own-first lookup as the displayed borrow (including JN1 chains and slot 7). A stale source gives “The shared source changed. Drag the chip again.” Confirmation also rechecks the approved slot kind and occupant, `Own(item)`, `Borrowed(item, lender)` or `Record(path)`. A changed destination gives “The destination slot changed. Drag the chip again.” Neither refusal writes (SC-8 to SC-10). A subtype-only edit can still leave the old slot kind in the last plan until its replacement lands (SC-12). A successful Share retargets downstream shares in the same Undo step (JN3) and preserves the JS7 normal edit.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JW6, 2026-11-20)
- **JW6 Remembered answer (A8).** `DropState.last_choice` holds the last Move, Share or Duplicate answered in Choice that the model accepted, including a choice leading to Replace. Cancel, Escape and a refused answer preserve it. It lasts for the app’s run across folder opens and is never saved.
  - In offered order the body buttons read “Move it here”, “Share (borrow its finished output)” (“Share (use this file here too, no copy)” for a raw helper), and “Duplicate the source for this set” when offered. A refused choice is disabled with its model reason on hover and in its AccessKit description. This includes Share for a raw helper onto an empty destination that needs a texture of its own (SC-5, SC-13). Choice caches the offers and reasons per opening, refreshing when the item, destination, model generation, views or editability changes (SC-17). `choice_refused` and the write in `drop_choice` both use `drop_precheck`.
  - A remembered choice offered by `drop_choices` with no refusal uses `selection.bg_fill`, the name “{label} (default, Enter)” and the hover “Your last choice. Enter picks it; hold Shift as you drop to skip this question.” The line below the buttons reads “Enter picks the highlighted choice. Hold Shift when you drop to skip this question.” With no usable answer there is no highlight or hint and Enter picks nothing.
  - Enter requires a previously open Choice and no focused widget (`dialogs::modal_keys`). Both Enter and Escape require Choice to be the top modal and no other dialog pending in the model, including one requested that frame. Escape wins over Enter in the same frame and leaves files, documents and Undo alone (SC-14, SC-16, SC-19).
  - Shift skips Choice only for an available remembered answer, rechecked at the drop, with exactly one primary release in the frame carrying Shift inside the owning card body, strip or bridge. Read that event’s modifiers and position. A later Shift change has no effect. With zero or multiple primary releases, or the Shift release outside the receiving zone, an ordinary drop that needs Choice still asks (JW1, SC-15, SC-20, SC-28, SC-30). Shift held before the press also works. A Textures tile range-selects on a Shift-click only, so a drag selects nothing.
  - **Amends FC5, K1 and K5 for the set-drop dialog only.** Replace always asks and needs a click. An accepted answer edits the open project documents. Duplicate also creates an image file immediately. Undo removes the copy. JU7’s disk-write keyboard proof still applies on other screens.

## Note HP — Sets column, second UI-proof round (main team, 2026-11-07)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **HP7 A cut title keeps the search match (SC4).** While a search is on and its folded text lies inside one middle segment (not the first, not the leaf), the title tries `…/<that segment>/<rest>` right after the whole id; a match in the first segment or the leaf, or across a `/`, uses HN6's order. **Amends HN6** while a search is on.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **HP9 Labels sit in one place (M2).** An empty chip draws its letter bottom-left, dimmed (HP8), where a filled chip draws it, and its AccessKit name is "<letter>, empty" ("G, empty"). A trailing-row tile paints a tag in the same spot: its slot letter when the slot is taken, "unused" for a helper with no fills, the first fill (`fill_text`, "M G") for a helper whose fills land on no chip, otherwise the member's tail ("_m"), or "—" when the tail is empty. A tag wider than the tile is cut with `…` ("CNR alpha", "RMAOS R"); the tile's hover keeps the whole text. **Amends HN2 and HN3.**

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **HP12 Proof fixes (UI proof `sets-column-hp`, SH3, SH4, SH6; owner answers 2026-11-08).** (a) SH4 (owner): when an open set takes Textures' room, Textures' shrunk width rounds up to whole tile columns (`columns::round_up_to_tiles`, called from `ui/mod.rs`), the extra room coming from Sets and never taking Sets below `SETS_MIN`; when the next whole column is out of Sets' reach the width is left as it is. At 1055x770 with a set open Textures is 259 px and holds 2 tiles per row (it held 1 beside a blank strip at 239 px); Sets is 240 px (it was 260). HP1's "5 chips per row" holds only for a Sets column of about 259 px or more, so a card with five chips wraps 4 + 4 at 1055x770 with a set open (it was 5 + 3). **Amends HP6** (the split). (b) SH6 (owner): the card's shader combo is capped at the row's inner width less 48 px, not at half; "Environment Map (auto)" shows whole on a 260 px column, and the 48 px on the row's left stay free card area. **Amends HP3** (owner answer 2's "half"). (c) SH3 (controller): a card's one-row heading needs `NAME_MIN.min(<the title's whole width>)` for the name, so a whole title that fits keeps its status pill on the title row (props/crate at 1055x770: pill y 251 below the title's 229.5, now on its row). **Amends FI13** (80 px, or the name's whole width when that is less). (d) Names match the painted text (egui-ui-rules 8.2; controller): the Sets shader filter button is "Filter, Sets shader filter" ("Filter (1), Sets shader filter: <shader>" with one chosen), and the trailing-row helper tile's name and hover say "unused: <reason>", the word its tag paints. The Textures helper rows and the helper panel paint "not used: …", so they keep it. **Amends HN7** (the filter's name) **and HN3** (the tile's hover).

Opening a set at 1055 narrows Sets only by what rounding Textures up to whole tile columns takes (HP12). In a narrow window opening a set moves the Sets column left once, by the room Textures gives up (owner accepted, 2026-11-08; 121 px at 1055x770 after HP12's rounding, 141 px before). **Amends FB2** (the split) **and HN4** ("FB2's narrowing is unchanged").

A tag wider than the tile is cut with `…` ("CNR alpha", "RMAOS R"); the tile's hover keeps the whole text. **Amends HN2 and HN3.**

## Note JP — A vanilla normal in a PBR set is one note, not two warnings; key-less sets and the grass source's class (owner, 2026-11-17)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note JS, 2026-11-18)
- **JP1 Engine (B3-1, B3-4, B3-6, B3-7, B3-14, B3-15).** `shader::check_maps`, on `pbr` and `pbr_grass` only (picked or automatic): when the required `_n` role is unfilled, the first own member typed `normal` or `landscape_normal` that fills no role answers it with one SHADER-NORMAL-SPECULAR (Info), in place of that member's SHADER-EXTRA-MAP and the `_n` SHADER-MISSING-MAP. Its data is `{"member", "shader", "map": "_n"}`, it comes after the set's other EXTRA and MISSING warnings, and its message reads "{member} is a vanilla normal: the {shader} shader reads it as _n but ignores any specular in its alpha; set its sub-type to pbr_normal to drop the alpha and let a _rmaos in the set be variance-baked from it (a DDS kept as it is keeps its alpha)".
  - A kept DDS whose suffix `suggest` types `normal` raises it too: `MemberMap` cannot tell a kept DDS from a converted source, and B3-14 adds no field. The message's last clause is true for both; the GUI words the kept row differently (JP3). `MemberMap`'s doc says a kept DDS has the sub-type `suggest` gives its suffix (Audit 08b-2).
  - Unchanged: PBR Landscape (it takes a vanilla normal as its `_n`, Note BM); `msn`, coat and detail normals; a vanilla normal beside a `pbr_normal`, and the second of two vanilla normals, which keep their SHADER-EXTRA-MAP; the vanilla target, where `pbr` falls back to `default`; derived picks, which have no map check (TA29).
  - The role table is not widened (B3-6): a filled `_n` would add Also outputs and a bake. So outputs, the bake, Also export and encode versions do not move, and the goldens stay byte-identical (B3-4).
  - `EVENT_SCHEMA` stays 2 (B3-15): adding a `Code` value adds an enum value, as OUT-KEPT-* (Note JA) and RESIZE-NO-REFERENCE (FM44) did without a bump. The digest line [S-shader-7, AZ4, AW4, JP1] states the rule. `event.schema.json` and `dds-validation.schema.json` list the value, and `SetResolved.warnings`' description names it.
  - Tests: `shader::tests::a_vanilla_normal_answers_pbr_n_with_an_info`, `msn_coat_and_detail_normals_still_miss_pbr_n`, `a_pbr_normal_beside_a_vanilla_one_keeps_the_extra`, `two_vanilla_normals_the_first_answers_n` and `pbr_landscape_default_and_derived_raise_no_info`; `shaders::an_own_plain_normal_in_a_pbr_set_is_one_info` and `a_kept_dds_normal_in_a_pbr_set_is_the_same_info`; the pins `bake_plan::an_own_plain_normal_in_a_pbr_set_keeps_its_alpha_and_does_not_bake`, `an_own_plain_normal_in_a_pbr_set_is_written_with_its_alpha` and `derive_plan::an_own_plain_normal_feeds_no_also_legacy_n`.
- **JP2 CLI (B3-8, B3-12).** FX1 holds: `sdc convert`'s log prints a set's warnings of severity warning or higher, so for that normal it prints neither the SHADER-EXTRA-MAP nor the `_n` SHADER-MISSING-MAP line, and not the Info (B3-12). `sdc inspect` and `inspect --json` show the Info. B3-2's "the CLI prints the same Info" is read through B3-8. Test `cli_inspect_validate::a_plain_normal_in_a_pbr_set_is_an_info_that_inspect_shows_and_convert_does_not`.
- **JP3 GUI (B3-2, B3-8, B3-9, B3-11, B3-13, B3-27).** `build_problems`' set loop makes a row for `makes_a_row`'s warnings and, by a named exception, for SHADER-NORMAL-SPECULAR: an Info result the user acts on. `row_class` classes it a Note, so its row is an Info note (JO1, JO3).
  - **Words (B3-9; planner's calls).** The row reads `code_text::note_sentence`'s "{member} is a vanilla normal: the {shader} shader ignores the specular in its alpha. Use PBR normal to drop the alpha and bake any _rmaos in this set from it."; with no `data.member` or `data.shader` it falls back to the engine message. `code_sentence` (the Kinds menu, `code_line`) is "A PBR set uses a vanilla normal, whose alpha the shader doesn't read — switch it to PBR normal". `names_its_map` lists the code (HC2). The words say the alpha drops and any `_rmaos` in the set bakes from it, which holds for a set with no `_rmaos` too (final review M1); the engine message says "variance-baked".
  - **Use PBR normal.** `Fix::UsePbrNormal(item)` runs `AppModel::use_pbr_normal`, which writes `subtype = "pbr_normal"` through `edit_subtype` as one undo step, "Use PBR normal" (`bottom::USE_PBR_NORMAL`). The button's accessible name is "Use PBR normal for {file}", since the row's sentence names the button too; its hover reads "Set its sub-type to PBR normal (subtype = "pbr_normal" in sdc.toml): its alpha is dropped, and any _rmaos in this set bakes from it, so distant surfaces shine less." Go to set outlines the normal (`SlotTarget::Item`, JO8). With no member of the set matching `data.member`, the row has no button and no target.
  - **Off (B3-11).** The button is off, with the reason on hover, while `fix_off` holds (a read-only `sdc.toml`, or a plan to wait for) and on a locked slot of the item's own set: `use_pbr_normal_refusal` reads `sets::leaves_locked` (`pub(crate)`), which gives `lock_refusal`'s "slot {n} of {set} is locked; unlock it first". `use_pbr_normal` refuses the same cases and writes nothing, and refuses an item no longer in the project. A set that borrows this normal into a locked slot of its own is not checked (`shortcut:` in `pbr_follow.rs`). The refusal is computed in `fix_click` at draw time, not cached: the problems cache key does not follow locks.
  - **A kept DDS (B3-11)**, a DDS kept as it is (no job, `TextureItem::kept_dds`), gets no button and its own sentence, "{member} is a kept DDS: the {shader} shader reads it as its normal and ignores the specular in its alpha." (`code_text::kept_normal_sentence`), so it is listed under **Notes** only (JO3); the model refuses it too ("{name} is kept as it is (a DDS is not converted again), so its type can't change its alpha"). A DDS set to be encoded again (`reencode = true`, FI3) has a job and is encoded like any source image, so its row is the plain one with the button, and the switch drops its alpha (final review I1). The engine's own Info on a kept DDS names the kept file's output name, which `extra_member` matches to the item's name.
  - **Keys (B3-27).** The button keeps Enter and Space (JO16): it has `RebuildZ`'s shape, not `click_only_button`, because it changes how a file is written, as **Tag as grass** and **Rebuild Z** do, and removes or switches off nothing. The row is an Info note, never a status-line reason (JO7's reasons are rows that count against Ready), so no reason button focuses it.
  - **Readiness (B3-13).** The Info never counts, and the set has no `_n` SHADER-MISSING-MAP and no "not used" note for that normal, only the one note (with **Use PBR normal** unless the normal is a kept DDS): with nothing else wrong it reads Ready. This is intended.
  - Tests: `problems_notes::the_set_loses_one_counted_problem_and_can_be_ready`, `use_pbr_normal_switches_the_type_in_one_click_and_one_undo_restores_it`, `use_pbr_normal_answers_enter_and_space`, `use_pbr_normal_is_off_on_a_locked_slot`, `use_pbr_normal_is_off_on_a_read_only_sdc_toml`, `a_kept_dds_normal_gets_the_note_without_a_button`, `the_engines_own_note_on_a_kept_dds_has_no_button`, `a_re_encoded_dds_normal_gets_the_note_with_its_button` and `no_specular_note_under_pbr_landscape`.
- **JP4 Borrowed and known limits (B3-3).**
  - A borrowed vanilla normal raises nothing new: `map_members` sees own members only, since the lender's file may need its alpha in its own set. HH8 (B1-6) is unchanged. Test `sets_model::a_borrowed_vanilla_normal_raises_no_specular_note`.
  - Known limit (plan review r1 m11): a set with its own vanilla normal that also resolves a share into slot 2. The engine still raises the Info for the own file, but the card shows the shared one in slot 2, so the row points at a file the card shows under the shared one. No test; upgrade when a user meets it.
  - Out of scope: Texalith and FaceGen Composer. A new `Code` value may need an arm there; the owner's call.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **JP5 A set with no engine key is named by its id (B3-16, B3-17, B3-19 to B3-23; F6 N1, N2, N4, N5).**
  - `bottom::scope_key` (the key, or the id when the key is empty) is what a set's Problems filter, its status-line reasons, "+k more in Problems", and the clicks of its card's **⚠ n** and check badge name it by. `scope` looks up the set with that key first, then the key-less set with that id, then the set whose id matches it by fold (`view_of`; Note BF: a keyed set's id is folded, a key-less one keeps its typed case), so a created set whose id equals another set's key resolves to the keyed set, and a filter opened on a key-less set's id follows the set once a file lands in it and it has a key (ids are root-relative and keys carry the output root, so the last lookup never meets a key; final review I2). A key-less scope matches the set rows built for that id and no other key-less set's. The `ProblemsFilter` set payload is a `String` holding a scope key (B3-19). The card's check badge still reads `set_check_warnings` by key, which is harmless: a key-less set has no check results.
  - `Flagged.keyless` holds the ids of key-less sets with a counted set row, apart from `Flagged.sets`, so an id never meets a key (planner's call). `bottom::no_files_yet` is a key-less set with no such row. `sets_column::pill_hover` takes the frame's `Flagged`, so the cards keep one `flagged` fetch a frame.
  - "Not ready: this set has no files yet." (`sets_column::NO_FILES_YET`) shows on the status line and the card's hover only for `no_files_yet`. Any other key-less set's status line reads "Not ready:" with its reasons, and its card's hover reads the "Not ready: a problem row…" line, as JO7 says (B3-17, B3-22).
  - A key-less set never reads Ready (`set_ready_in` unchanged), so a key-less set whose every counted row is marked reads "no files yet" (B3-21).
  - Known limits (B3-20, parked with F2 in Plan 3b): the editor opens the first key-less set for `go_to_set("")`, and a key-less card cannot be clicked, so only the first key-less set in view order shows its status line until sets are selected by id; the model API is exact for every key-less set. A Set filter on a key-less set goes stale when the set is renamed while the filter is open (B3-19), and nothing clears it.
  - Related wording (B3-22, B3-23): JO7's "Reasons" cache key, JO10 (N2) and JO15's mark and count sentence (N4) state these rules. `ui/messages.rs`'s `shortcut:` comment gives the true cost of `request_discard`, which is not used there. `pbr_export::the_written_note_counts_the_entries` states its fixture (N5).
  - Tests: `problems_notes::a_key_less_set_lists_and_reasons_its_own_rows_only`, `a_key_less_set_with_rows_reads_not_ready_on_its_card`, `a_set_with_no_entry_says_no_files_yet_on_its_status_line`, `a_key_less_sets_filter_follows_the_set_once_it_has_a_key`, `a_key_less_sets_filter_follows_a_mixed_case_id`, `a_key_less_sets_reason_opens_its_own_rows`, and the kept `a_set_with_no_files_yet_says_so_on_its_card`.

ARCHIVED (JP5 wording before F11 fix 1, superseded in part by Note JV, 2026-11-19)
  - `bottom::scope_key` (the key, or the id when the key is empty) is what a set's Problems filter, "+k more in Problems", and the clicks of its card's **⚠ n** and check badge name it by. The editor reads its status-line reasons from the resolved view (JV1). A row-reason button opens Problems with that view's typed key or id and focuses its own row, even for an empty id. An ordinary empty filter string names no rows. For an ordinary filter, `scope` looks up the set with that key first, then the key-less set with that id, then the set whose id matches it by fold, then the key-less set whose key would be that key (`view_of`; Note BF: a keyed set's id is folded, a key-less one keeps its typed case; the fourth lookup is JR11's), so a created set whose id equals another set's key resolves to the keyed set, and a filter opened on a nonempty key-less id follows the set when a file lands unless that string resolves first as another set's key. A key-less scope matches the set rows built for that id and no other key-less set's. The `ProblemsFilter` set payload is a `String` holding a scope key (B3-19). The card's check badge still reads `set_check_warnings` by key, which is harmless: a key-less set has no check results.

ARCHIVED (JP5 wording before the F11 edit, superseded in part by Note JV, 2026-11-19)
  - `bottom::scope_key` (the key, or the id when the key is empty) is what a set's Problems filter, its status-line reasons, "+k more in Problems", and the clicks of its card's **⚠ n** and check badge name it by. `scope` looks up the set with that key first, then the key-less set with that id, then the set whose id matches it by fold, then the key-less set whose key would be that key (`view_of`; Note BF: a keyed set's id is folded, a key-less one keeps its typed case; the fourth lookup is JR11's), so a created set whose id equals another set's key resolves to the keyed set, and a filter opened on a key-less set's id follows the set once a file lands in it and it has a key (ids are root-relative and keys carry the output root, so the fold lookup never meets a key; final review I2). A key-less scope matches the set rows built for that id and no other key-less set's. The `ProblemsFilter` set payload is a `String` holding a scope key (B3-19). The card's check badge still reads `set_check_warnings` by key, which is harmless: a key-less set has no check results.
  - Known limits (B3-20, parked with F2 in Plan 3b): the editor opens the first key-less set for `go_to_set("")`, and a key-less card cannot be clicked, so only the first key-less set in view order shows its status line until sets are selected by id; the model API is exact for every key-less set. A Set filter on a key-less set goes stale when the set is renamed while the filter is open (B3-19), and nothing clears it.
  - Related wording (B3-22, B3-23): JO7's "Reasons" cache key, JO10 (N2) and JO15's mark and count sentence (N4) state these rules. `ui/messages.rs`'s `shortcut:` comment gives the true cost of `request_discard`, which is not used there. `pbr_export::the_written_note_counts_the_entries` states its fixture (N5).

## Note JR — Sets and slots: links, outputs, labels, titles (review E plan 3b, 2026-11-16)

ARCHIVED (JR14 and JR17 before ruling 3b-F47, 2026-11-19)
- **JR14 Export reads what the JSON carries (3b-F3, F12, F13, F18, F19, F23, F35, F41 to F45).** The no-link, no-clear guard pins the export blocks and written JSON bytes for its project (`partial_export::with_no_link_and_no_clear_export_blocks_and_writes_what_it_did`). Membership identifies a pack's owner even when the set borrows slot 6. Mapping that pack to its default path creates no collision with itself. A consumer borrowing the pack can name the lender's legacy output name. JR17 gives the slot path and the checks Export makes before writing the entry.
  - **Path identity and the index.** Export, From another set and the index use the same projected game path (JR17). The index includes each set member that writes its own output, including extra files of the same kind and unslotted files. The first producer in display order wins. An unslotted complex `_m` supplies a file without turning on a feature. PGPatcher folds ASCII case and adds `textures\` when the stored string lacks it. The index uses that rule to look up a stored `slotN`. Path… validates a relative DDS path and stores backslashes. From another set offers members with a game location and logs a changed pick (JR1). The menu waits for planning and scan reads to finish and for the displayed plan to match the current settings. A failed plan holds the picker and Export. The menu caches the settled check until its inputs change and the click repeats it before storing the path. The menu and index each read the output roots once per list. When a new plan installs different game paths, the index resolves the links again.
  - **One answer to "does this set write an entry".** `pbr_export::writes_entry` is `exports_json` (CS, a JSON set, a match that applies) and the statics rule keeping the entry (`pgp_entry_wanted`; a CS set with statics set to Never writes none). The link display, the feature defaults (`features`), the missing-map suppression and the cleared state read it through `link_slots`, and `written_json_entries` filters by it. On a set that writes no entry a stored link or clear changes no feature default and drops no missing-map row, and an own file under `slotN = ""` shows as itself (no dim chip, no "Left out of the JSON" sentence): `slot_shown`'s `Cleared` needs the file left out of `features`. `link_slots` asks `writes_entry` only of a set with a `slotN` string. The one exception is a record fill (controller ruling 3b-F13, withdrawing 3b-F9's record-fill clause): a stored `slotN` of any value keeps a TXST record from filling that slot (Note HG) on every set, a set that writes no entry included, as on main, because that decision feeds the engine's `borrowed_normal` input and so the roughness bake, and encoded bytes do not change. So on a statics-Never mapped set a typed path or a hand-edited `slotN` over a record-provided fill hides that fill in the editor, as on main (`slot_links::a_stored_slot_command_keeps_a_record_fill_out_as_on_main`, `txst_map::a_stored_slot_key_keeps_the_record_normal_out_of_the_engine_inputs`). Readers that promise the written result read `writes_entry` too: the no-albedo Info row; the 3D preview's candidate entry leaves the command out (JR7).
  - **Validation.** Export's blocks read what the JSON names (`sets::json_files`, over the fields the feature keys read: `feature_slots` and `linked`), in three states: a local file (an own or borrowed fill no `slotN` overrides, or a link that names a set's output, on the row it fills), a typed path no set writes (it fills its game slot for the JSON and is never missing), or nothing (a cleared slot). So "{feature} is on but the set has no …" counts a linked or typed-path height, coat normal, fuzz, glow or subsurface and does not count a cleared one; "_cnr and _f both use slot 7" and "_g and _sk both use slot 3" need two own or borrowed files the JSON keeps, so a `slotN` on that slot (a link, a typed path or a clear) clears the clash; and the "has no output path" check skips a slot whose `slotN` the JSON writes, a clear included (`slots_of` writes the string). One helper answers "does the JSON keep this fill" for the clash, the output-path check and the editor's conflict line with its Remove buttons (`sets::json_keeps_fill`, read by `view_row` without allocating), so on every set the editor shows a conflict only where the JSON keeps both fills: on a set that writes an entry, in the run or not, that is its effective slots (a `slotN` on the slot leaves no conflict); on a set that writes none (its statics drop it), its own and borrowed fills. Export checks a set in the run in its blocks, and a set left out of the run only in `prepare` and only when it has a stored slot command (its entry is held with the reason); a set left out with none is not checked (controller rulings 3b-F8, 3b-F12 and 3b-F17; `set_editor_ui::a_slot_the_json_overrides_shows_no_conflict_as_export_has_none`, `slot_links::export_takes_a_typed_path_over_a_clashing_slot_7_or_3`). On a set that writes no entry these read its own and borrowed fills, as before. The typed-path state changes a block for a set whose `slotN` names no set's output: Parallax on with a typed slot-4 path no set writes no longer blocks, and a typed slot-7 path over an own `_cnr` and `_f` no longer clashes.
  - **Dependencies and scope.** A link that names a set's output is a dependency exactly as a borrow is; scope and dependencies come from the link as `link_slots` resolved it, so a `slotN` string that names no settled output (a typed path, or a path the producer no longer writes) is an external path with no host and no dependency (controller ruling 3b-F5). Export set, Export group and Export N sets include local lenders under the same excluded-group rule. A direct queued export expands the selected keys after planning settles. If Export in Review triggers a source rescan, it keeps the keys expanded before the rescan. A newly resolved lender can stay outside the run and its consumer is held when the file is missing (3b-F20, 3b-F36). The finalizer's dependencies of a JSON entry are the local files `json_files` names (`pbr_export::prepare`). Export compares the emitted local slot with the producer's projected game path (JR17) and matches the run row by its manifest job key. For an excluded local lender, Export checks the job's physical output at its exact spelling, including Unicode. For a legacy height written outside `textures` with no game location, Export also checks the file named by the derived slot (JR17). A failed row holds the entry even if an older file exists. A Vanilla borrow copy checks its captured source path (3b-F19). A linked file is checked like a borrowed one (written or up to date in this run, or on disk for a set left out on purpose, HF9) and the entry is held with the same note when it is not. An own or borrowed file that a `slotN` (a link, a typed path or a clear) overrides is not in the JSON, so it is no dependency of the entry; the set's own conversion jobs and a borrow's scope are unchanged. So a clear over a borrowed file never exported lets the entry in (`partial_export::a_clear_over_a_never_exported_borrow_lets_the_entry_in`). An entry of a set left out of the run gates nothing (HF9). A set with a stored slot command is written only when its own checks pass (the clash, missing-texture, feature-clash, prefix, slot-path and output-path blocks, `pbr_export::set_blocks`); otherwise it is held with "not in JSON: {set} ({reason})" and the run goes on. A set with none is not checked, as on main: its files on disk are accepted and its entry is written (controller rulings 3b-F7 and 3b-F12; `partial_export::an_entry_left_out_of_the_run_is_held_when_its_own_checks_refuse_it`, `…::with_no_slot_command_an_excluded_set_is_written_as_main_writes_it`). Tests `partial_export::a_scoped_export_treats_a_linked_lender_as_a_borrowed_one`, `an_excluded_linked_lender_holds_the_entry_as_a_borrowed_one_does` and `a_borrow_under_a_link_is_no_dependency`.
  - **The no-albedo Info.** Decided from the JSON the writer produces: an explicit `slot1` (a link, a typed path or a clear) is what the JSON names for slot 1, so the mesh keeps nothing and the "No albedo in this set; the mesh keeps {path}…" row (HL6) is not raised; `pbr_export::no_albedo_slot1` is `None` then, and `slots_of` writes the same JSON as before (the explicit `slot1` won already). Test `slot_links::with_a_linked_slot_1_the_no_albedo_info_is_not_raised`.
- **JR17 Written slot paths (3b-F35, F39 to F43 and F45).** The landed plan supplies the game directory from the accepted TXST mapping, the owning PBR set's output prefix or the source job's game path. The job supplies the filename it writes. A mapping to `textures\armor\textures\stone_p.dds` keeps both the `armor` directory and its inner `textures` directory. The JSON can omit the leading `textures\` when PGPatcher resolves the string to the same file.
  - For an owned PBR albedo with no slot command, Export compares the output and default paths using Unicode lowercase. With `match1 = 'stone\É'` and `rename = 'textures\pbr\stone\é'`, the JSON omits `slot1`. An explicit height can keep its source game path `textures\armor\rock_p.dds`, and a renamed albedo keeps the interior dot in `textures\stone\.\wall.dds`. If a TXST requests `textures\İ\rock_fuzz.dds` and the job writes `rock_f.dds`, the lender and borrower both name `textures\İ\rock_f.dds`.
  - For an emitted slot supplied by this project's own file, a borrow or a stored local link, Export checks the string PGPatcher reads against the producer's projected location. A mismatch holds the entry. The producer also needs a completed or up-to-date row, or its physical DDS on disk when left out of the run. A failed row holds the entry even over an old DDS. A legacy height written outside `textures` also needs its installed copy, including when `lock_parallax = true` and `slot4 = false`. A typed or retained external path has no local producer and passes through as written.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-19)
- **JR14 Export reads what the JSON carries (3b-F3, F12, F13, F18, F19, F23, F35, F41 and F42).** The no-link, no-clear guard pins the export blocks and written JSON bytes for its project (`partial_export::with_no_link_and_no_clear_export_blocks_and_writes_what_it_did`). Membership identifies a pack's owner even when the set borrows slot 6. Mapping that pack to its default path creates no collision with itself. A consumer borrowing the pack can name the lender's legacy output name. JR17 describes the mapped height and fuzz paths, the file checks for an excluded lender and when an owned albedo needs a slot command.
  - **Path identity and the index.** A link is looked up with PGPatcher's own explicit-slot rule (`pgpsim` `slots.rs`, the `slot1`..`slot8` pass): ASCII lower case, `textures\` put in front unless the string starts with it (`txst_map::slot_path_key`), so `pbr\arch\wall_n.dds` and `textures\pbr\arch\wall_n.dds` are one file and `other\textures\pbr\arch\wall_n.dds` is not that file. Note HG's record paths keep `game_key`. The index (`txst_map::written_outputs`) holds every planned output of every set (each member that writes a file of its own: the members after the first of a slot kind and the outputs that fill no slot included), built in one pass with each output's kind (`slot_of`) and its set's short name; so on a settled plan every pick the From another set menu offers names an index key, and on a consumer that writes an entry (JR1) it is a link to the set that writes that path (the first in display order when two sets write it) and logs its Info line only on a changed pick (JR1). An output that fills no slot (a complex `_m`) is a local file with no kind, so it counts for no feature (JR8 needs a kind). An index key is the game location of the file the settled job writes on disk (`sets::written_game_location`): the job's output under the output folder or the export folder, its filesystem components (`.` and empty parts gone, as the writer resolves them) from the first `textures` component on (ASCII case-insensitive), joined with backslashes and ASCII-lowercased. The explicit path a mapping or prefix asks for is not read: the engine may settle on another file name (a mapped `rock_fuzz.png` typed `pbr_fuzz` writes `rock_f.dds`). It never starts from the manifest's job key (Unicode-lowercased, which loses a folder such as `İ`) and never splits a path as text. Path… strips leading separators, validates the relative DDS path and stores slashes as backslashes (3b-F32). A hand-written `slotN` goes straight to the explicit-slot lookup. From another set stores this path, spelt as on disk, and only on a settled plan (controller ruling 3b-F21, which withdraws 3b-F16's displayed prediction): `AppModel::pick_waits` waits on what Export waits on before it checks anything, a full plan or a scan-database read out (`full_plan_pending`, behind which `export_go` queues the export) and a plan that is not the one for these sets (`inputs_unsettled`, on which `plan_unsettled` refuses the export). After terminal delivery `plan_failed` holds picks and both export buttons until another plan starts. Export refuses before source or scope checks with “The last plan failed. Reopen the folder to plan it again before exporting.” This covers inputs equal to the failed request. The flag can also stay set after a later non-planning edit changes those inputs (3b-F27, 3b-F32). While it waits, every member row is off with one reason and no member's path is read, and a click (which checks again, `member_game_path`) stores nothing. The reason says which wait it is (controller ruling 3b-F25): "Planning: pick a file once the plan has finished" while a plan or a scan-database read runs, and "The plan must run again first: pick a file once a new plan has finished" when none runs but the plan is out of date or failed (a Path edit that changes a borrowed normal starts no plan. Picks and Export wait until another edit replans, 3b-F32). The rows read `pick_waits_cached` (controller ruling 3b-F24): a running plan or read is checked live, and the rest is computed once per `PickKey` (the views key, the plan generation, `sdc.toml`'s revision, `evidence_rev`, `mesh_rev` and `plan_failed`), so a menu kept open on frames where none of that changes builds no export inputs; the click's check in `member_game_path` is never cached. Export also plans again first when a source changed on disk (`check_sources`); a pick does not look at the files. The path comes from the settled job's output alone (`member_game_path_in`), never from an explicit path a pending edit asks for, so a pick never stores a path the producer does not write and an output with no game location keeps its error. The index never holds a prediction either, and `view_reads` holds each job's output, so a link resolved before a plan lands is resolved again when the output moves (controller ruling 3b-F16; `slot_links::a_link_is_recomputed_when_the_plan_moves_its_output_back`). A path is never composed from a source folder and an output name, and `slot_path_key` is applied on the lookup side only. The index finds the set that writes an output by membership (it walks each set's members), and the path function reads only the two folders and the item's settled output (`member_game_path_in(roots, item)`), so a packed output whose set borrows its slot, or one mapped to another folder, keeps its key. An output under no `textures` folder (a legacy `output_name`) has no game location: it is in no entry, and From another set disables it (`NO_GAME_PATH`). The member list and the index read the folders through `sets::output_roots` (one borrowed read of the cached export configuration and one of the export folder, over `AppModel::output_root_in`), once per menu call or index build, never per member; the export inputs read them once per pass, not per item (`export_rel_in`, `export_key_in`). A warm Assign call, including the row-cache key, copies zero `[export].skip` IDs (3b-F29). Reselecting the same folder changes the generation and terminal failure changes `plan_failed`, each invalidating the key even when the request inputs match (3b-F30). The read-count test pins a warm menu call with members at four reads of each with an Also tick on CS (two views lookups, the rows' cache key and `output_roots`), the same with more members and items; while a plan is out the list adds no read beyond the views lookups and builds no export inputs (controller rulings 3b-F6, 3b-F11, 3b-F14, 3b-F21 and 3b-F24, which withdraws 3b-F21's once-per-call bound; `slot_links::the_output_index_holds_where_each_file_is_written`, ten layouts through a real export, checked again with the consumer exported; `slot_links::the_slot_menu_builds_the_export_inputs_once_per_call`, `…::the_menu_and_the_index_read_the_folders_once_whatever_the_members`, `…::a_member_pick_waits_for_the_plan_and_then_stores_the_written_path`, `…::a_removed_mapping_offers_no_old_path_before_or_after_the_plan`, `…::a_member_pick_after_a_failed_replan_is_refused`, `…::an_open_slot_menu_builds_the_settled_check_once`, `…::the_settled_check_is_built_again_after_each_change_of_its_key`, `…::the_click_checks_the_plan_again_without_the_rows_cache`, `txst_map::with_the_plan_out_of_date_and_none_running_a_member_pick_says_it_must_plan_again`, `slot_menu::the_game_path_follows_the_current_prefix_not_the_last_plan`).

- **JR17 Written paths for owned and borrowed slots (3b-F35, F39, F40, F41 and F42).** Export reads the settled job's path relative to the output folder. - **JR14 Export reads what the JSON carries (3b-F3, F12, F13, F18, F19, F23, F35 and F41).** The no-link, no-clear guard pins the export blocks and written JSON bytes for its project (`partial_export::with_no_link_and_no_clear_export_blocks_and_writes_what_it_did`). Membership identifies a pack's owner even when the set borrows slot 6. Mapping that pack to its default path creates no collision with itself. A consumer borrowing the pack can name the lender's legacy output name. JR17 covers the DDS files a local lender must supply and the spelling of the published slot.
  - **Path identity and the index.** A link is looked up with PGPatcher's own explicit-slot rule (`pgpsim` `slots.rs`, the `slot1`..`slot8` pass): ASCII lower case, `textures\` put in front unless the string starts with it (`txst_map::slot_path_key`), so `pbr\arch\wall_n.dds` and `textures\pbr\arch\wall_n.dds` are one file and `other\textures\pbr\arch\wall_n.dds` is not that file. Note HG's record paths keep `game_key`. The index (`txst_map::written_outputs`) holds every planned output of every set (each member that writes a file of its own: the members after the first of a slot kind and the outputs that fill no slot included), built in one pass with each output's kind (`slot_of`) and its set's short name; so on a settled plan every pick the From another set menu offers names an index key, and on a consumer that writes an entry (JR1) it is a link to the set that writes that path (the first in display order when two sets write it) and logs its Info line only on a changed pick (JR1). An output that fills no slot (a complex `_m`) is a local file with no kind, so it counts for no feature (JR8 needs a kind). An index key is the game location of the file the settled job writes on disk (`sets::written_game_location`): the job's output under the output folder or the export folder, its filesystem components (`.` and empty parts gone, as the writer resolves them) from the first `textures` component on (ASCII case-insensitive), joined with backslashes and ASCII-lowercased. The explicit path a mapping or prefix asks for is not read: the engine may settle on another file name (a mapped `rock_fuzz.png` typed `pbr_fuzz` writes `rock_f.dds`). It never starts from the manifest's job key (Unicode-lowercased, which loses a folder such as `İ`) and never splits a path as text. Path… strips leading separators, validates the relative DDS path and stores slashes as backslashes (3b-F32). A hand-written `slotN` goes straight to the explicit-slot lookup. From another set stores this path, spelt as on disk, and only on a settled plan (controller ruling 3b-F21, which withdraws 3b-F16's displayed prediction): `AppModel::pick_waits` waits on what Export waits on before it checks anything, a full plan or a scan-database read out (`full_plan_pending`, behind which `export_go` queues the export) and a plan that is not the one for these sets (`inputs_unsettled`, on which `plan_unsettled` refuses the export). After terminal delivery `plan_failed` holds picks and both export buttons until another plan starts. Export refuses before source or scope checks with “The last plan failed. Reopen the folder to plan it again before exporting.” This covers inputs equal to the failed request. The flag can also stay set after a later non-planning edit changes those inputs (3b-F27, 3b-F32). While it waits, every member row is off with one reason and no member's path is read, and a click (which checks again, `member_game_path`) stores nothing. The reason says which wait it is (controller ruling 3b-F25): "Planning: pick a file once the plan has finished" while a plan or a scan-database read runs, and "The plan must run again first: pick a file once a new plan has finished" when none runs but the plan is out of date or failed (a Path edit that changes a borrowed normal starts no plan. Picks and Export wait until another edit replans, 3b-F32). The rows read `pick_waits_cached` (controller ruling 3b-F24): a running plan or read is checked live, and the rest is computed once per `PickKey` (the views key, the plan generation, `sdc.toml`'s revision, `evidence_rev`, `mesh_rev` and `plan_failed`), so a menu kept open on frames where none of that changes builds no export inputs; the click's check in `member_game_path` is never cached. Export also plans again first when a source changed on disk (`check_sources`); a pick does not look at the files. The path comes from the settled job's output alone (`member_game_path_in`), never from an explicit path a pending edit asks for, so a pick never stores a path the producer does not write and an output with no game location keeps its error. The index never holds a prediction either, and `view_reads` holds each job's output, so a link resolved before a plan lands is resolved again when the output moves (controller ruling 3b-F16; `slot_links::a_link_is_recomputed_when_the_plan_moves_its_output_back`). A path is never composed from a source folder and an output name, and `slot_path_key` is applied on the lookup side only. The index finds the set that writes an output by membership (it walks each set's members), and the path function reads only the two folders and the item's settled output (`member_game_path_in(roots, item)`), so a packed output whose set borrows its slot, or one mapped to another folder, keeps its key. An output under no `textures` folder (a legacy `output_name`) has no game location: it is in no entry, and From another set disables it (`NO_GAME_PATH`). The member list and the index read the folders through `sets::output_roots` (one borrowed read of the cached export configuration and one of the export folder, over `AppModel::output_root_in`), once per menu call or index build, never per member; the export inputs read them once per pass, not per item (`export_rel_in`, `export_key_in`). A warm Assign call, including the row-cache key, copies zero `[export].skip` IDs (3b-F29). Reselecting the same folder changes the generation and terminal failure changes `plan_failed`, each invalidating the key even when the request inputs match (3b-F30). The read-count test pins a warm menu call with members at four reads of each with an Also tick on CS (two views lookups, the rows' cache key and `output_roots`), the same with more members and items; while a plan is out the list adds no read beyond the views lookups and builds no export inputs (controller rulings 3b-F6, 3b-F11, 3b-F14, 3b-F21 and 3b-F24, which withdraws 3b-F21's once-per-call bound; `slot_links::the_output_index_holds_where_each_file_is_written`, ten layouts through a real export, checked again with the consumer exported; `slot_links::the_slot_menu_builds_the_export_inputs_once_per_call`, `…::the_menu_and_the_index_read_the_folders_once_whatever_the_members`, `…::a_member_pick_waits_for_the_plan_and_then_stores_the_written_path`, `…::a_removed_mapping_offers_no_old_path_before_or_after_the_plan`, `…::a_member_pick_after_a_failed_replan_is_refused`, `…::an_open_slot_menu_builds_the_settled_check_once`, `…::the_settled_check_is_built_again_after_each_change_of_its_key`, `…::the_click_checks_the_plan_again_without_the_rows_cache`, `txst_map::with_the_plan_out_of_date_and_none_running_a_member_pick_says_it_must_plan_again`, `slot_menu::the_game_path_follows_the_current_prefix_not_the_last_plan`).
  - **One answer to "does this set write an entry".** `pbr_export::writes_entry` is `exports_json` (CS, a JSON set, a match that applies) and the statics rule keeping the entry (`pgp_entry_wanted`; a CS set with statics set to Never writes none). The link display, the feature defaults (`features`), the missing-map suppression and the cleared state read it through `link_slots`, and `written_json_entries` filters by it. On a set that writes no entry a stored link or clear changes no feature default and drops no missing-map row, and an own file under `slotN = ""` shows as itself (no dim chip, no "Left out of the JSON" sentence): `slot_shown`'s `Cleared` needs the file left out of `features`. `link_slots` asks `writes_entry` only of a set with a `slotN` string. The one exception is a record fill (controller ruling 3b-F13, withdrawing 3b-F9's record-fill clause): a stored `slotN` of any value keeps a TXST record from filling that slot (Note HG) on every set, a set that writes no entry included, as on main, because that decision feeds the engine's `borrowed_normal` input and so the roughness bake, and encoded bytes do not change. So on a statics-Never mapped set a typed path or a hand-edited `slotN` over a record-provided fill hides that fill in the editor, as on main (`slot_links::a_stored_slot_command_keeps_a_record_fill_out_as_on_main`, `txst_map::a_stored_slot_key_keeps_the_record_normal_out_of_the_engine_inputs`). Readers that promise the written result read `writes_entry` too: the no-albedo Info row; the 3D preview's candidate entry leaves the command out (JR7).
  - **Validation.** Export's blocks read what the JSON names (`sets::json_files`, over the fields the feature keys read: `feature_slots` and `linked`), in three states: a local file (an own or borrowed fill no `slotN` overrides, or a link that names a set's output, on the row it fills), a typed path no set writes (it fills its game slot for the JSON and is never missing), or nothing (a cleared slot). So "{feature} is on but the set has no …" counts a linked or typed-path height, coat normal, fuzz, glow or subsurface and does not count a cleared one; "_cnr and _f both use slot 7" and "_g and _sk both use slot 3" need two own or borrowed files the JSON keeps, so a `slotN` on that slot (a link, a typed path or a clear) clears the clash; and the "has no output path" check skips a slot whose `slotN` the JSON writes, a clear included (`slots_of` writes the string). One helper answers "does the JSON keep this fill" for the clash, the output-path check and the editor's conflict line with its Remove buttons (`sets::json_keeps_fill`, read by `view_row` without allocating), so on every set the editor shows a conflict only where the JSON keeps both fills: on a set that writes an entry, in the run or not, that is its effective slots (a `slotN` on the slot leaves no conflict); on a set that writes none (its statics drop it), its own and borrowed fills. Export checks a set in the run in its blocks, and a set left out of the run only in `prepare` and only when it has a stored slot command (its entry is held with the reason); a set left out with none is not checked (controller rulings 3b-F8, 3b-F12 and 3b-F17; `set_editor_ui::a_slot_the_json_overrides_shows_no_conflict_as_export_has_none`, `slot_links::export_takes_a_typed_path_over_a_clashing_slot_7_or_3`). On a set that writes no entry these read its own and borrowed fills, as before. The typed-path state changes a block for a set whose `slotN` names no set's output: Parallax on with a typed slot-4 path no set writes no longer blocks, and a typed slot-7 path over an own `_cnr` and `_f` no longer clashes.
  - **Dependencies and scope.** A link that names a set's output is a dependency exactly as a borrow is; scope and dependencies come from the link as `link_slots` resolved it, so a `slotN` string that names no settled output (a typed path, or a path the producer no longer writes) is an external path with no host and no dependency (controller ruling 3b-F5). Export set, Export group and Export N sets include local lenders under the same excluded-group rule. A direct queued export expands the selected keys after planning settles. If Export in Review triggers a source rescan, it keeps the keys expanded before the rescan. A newly resolved lender can stay outside the run and its consumer is held when the file is missing (3b-F20, 3b-F36). The finalizer's dependencies of a JSON entry are the local files `json_files` names (`pbr_export::prepare`). The run row is matched by its manifest job key. For an excluded local lender, Export checks the job's physical output at its exact spelling, including Unicode. If a legacy job writes outside the slot's game location, both files must exist (JR17). A failed row holds the entry even if an older file exists. A Vanilla borrow copy checks its captured source path (3b-F19). A linked file is checked like a borrowed one (written or up to date in this run, or on disk for a set left out on purpose, HF9) and the entry is held with the same note when it is not. An own or borrowed file that a `slotN` (a link, a typed path or a clear) overrides is not in the JSON, so it is no dependency of the entry; the set's own conversion jobs and a borrow's scope are unchanged. So a clear over a borrowed file never exported lets the entry in (`partial_export::a_clear_over_a_never_exported_borrow_lets_the_entry_in`). An entry of a set left out of the run gates nothing (HF9). A set with a stored slot command is written only when its own checks pass (the clash, missing-texture, feature-clash, prefix, slot-path and output-path blocks, `pbr_export::set_blocks`); otherwise it is held with "not in JSON: {set} ({reason})" and the run goes on. A set with none is not checked, as on main: its files on disk are accepted and its entry is written (controller rulings 3b-F7 and 3b-F12; `partial_export::an_entry_left_out_of_the_run_is_held_when_its_own_checks_refuse_it`, `…::with_no_slot_command_an_excluded_set_is_written_as_main_writes_it`). Tests `partial_export::a_scoped_export_treats_a_linked_lender_as_a_borrowed_one`, `an_excluded_linked_lender_holds_the_entry_as_a_borrowed_one_does` and `a_borrow_under_a_link_is_no_dependency`.
  - **The no-albedo Info.** Decided from the JSON the writer produces: an explicit `slot1` (a link, a typed path or a clear) is what the JSON names for slot 1, so the mesh keeps nothing and the "No albedo in this set; the mesh keeps {path}…" row (HL6) is not raised; `pbr_export::no_albedo_slot1` is `None` then, and `slots_of` writes the same JSON as before (the explicit `slot1` won already). Test `slot_links::with_a_linked_slot_1_the_no_albedo_info_is_not_raised`.

- **JR17 Written paths for owned and borrowed slots (3b-F35, 3b-F39, 3b-F40, 3b-F41).** Export reads the settled DDS path relative to the output folder. A staging folder above it cannot enter the slot. When the output folder is the input's own `textures` directory, the game path includes that directory.
  - The slot keeps the requested spelling when it resolves to the written DDS. For example, `textures\Armor\rock_p.png` keeps its source game path `textures\armor\rock_p.dds`, and `textures\stone\.\wall.dds` keeps its interior dot. If a TXST requests `textures\İ\rock_fuzz.dds` and the job writes `rock_f.dds`, the lender and borrower both name `textures\İ\rock_f.dds`.
  - A completed or up-to-date row supplies a DDS at the emitted game location. Export checks an excluded lender's physical DDS under its mod folder. When a legacy job writes `arch\stone_p.dds` and the slot resolves to `textures\arch\stone_p.dds`, Export requires both files, even after a successful row. This includes the derived height written with `lock_parallax = true` and `slot4 = false`. A failed row holds the entry even if an older DDS exists.

A set with a stored slot command is written only when its own checks pass (the clash, missing-texture, feature-clash, prefix, slot-path and output-path blocks, `pbr_export::set_blocks`); otherwise it is held with "not in JSON: {set} ({reason})" and the run goes on. A set with none is not checked, as on main: its files on disk are accepted and its entry is written (controller rulings 3b-F7 and 3b-F12; `partial_export::an_entry_left_out_of_the_run_is_held_when_its_own_checks_refuse_it`, `…::with_no_slot_command_an_excluded_set_is_written_as_main_writes_it`). Tests `partial_export::a_scoped_export_treats_a_linked_lender_as_a_borrowed_one`, `an_excluded_linked_lender_holds_the_entry_as_a_borrowed_one_does` and `a_borrow_under_a_link_is_no_dependency`.
  - **The no-albedo Info.** Decided from the JSON the writer produces: an explicit `slot1` (a link, a typed path or a clear) is what the JSON names for slot 1, so the mesh keeps nothing and the "No albedo in this set; the mesh keeps {path}…" row (HL6) is not raised; `pbr_export::no_albedo_slot1` is `None` then, and `slots_of` writes the same JSON as before (the explicit `slot1` won already). Test `slot_links::with_a_linked_slot_1_the_no_albedo_info_is_not_raised`.

- **JR17 Written paths for owned and borrowed slots (3b-F35, 3b-F39, 3b-F40).** An owned or borrowed slot keeps its requested spelling when that path names the written DDS. For example, `textures\Armor\rock_p.png` keeps its source game path `textures\armor\rock_p.dds` in the JSON. If a TXST requests `textures\İ\rock_fuzz.dds` and the job writes `rock_f.dds`, both the lender and the set borrowing slot 7 name `textures\İ\rock_f.dds`.
  - A completed or up-to-date job supplies its file. For a set left out of the run, Export checks each job's physical output, including its mod folder. A failed job holds the entry even when an older file exists.
  - A legacy output outside `textures` can leave the written slot without a producer at its game path. Export checks that game file before publishing the entry. This also applies when `lock_parallax = true` and `slot4 = false` allow a derived height path into the JSON. The entry is held until the named file exists.

ARCHIVED (JR17 before ruling 3b-F40, 2026-11-19)
- **JR17 Written paths for owned and borrowed slots (3b-F35, 3b-F39).** When the JSON names an owned or borrowed file, its slot uses the game location of the DDS the settled job writes. If a TXST maps a fuzz texture to `textures\İ\rock_fuzz.dds` and the job writes `textures\İ\rock_f.dds`, both the lender and the set borrowing its slot 7 use `rock_f.dds` in their JSON entries. When the lender is excluded, Export checks the file at that exact spelling. If no job writes the named game path, Export checks that path under the output folder. A missing file or a failed conversion holds the affected entry.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **JR1 A `slotN` with a non-empty value is a link (E1).** It is shown only on a set that exports a JSON entry (`pbr_export::exports_json`). A link sits on the row of its game slot (`link.row`): the source item's kind when that kind's game slot is the link's slot; else the kind the set already has on that slot; else the slot's default kind (1 Diffuse, 2 Normal, 3 Emissive, 4 Height, 6 RMAOS, 7 Coat, 8 Subsurface; `sets::link_row_kind`). On game slot 5 with no kind of the set there it has no row. So a slot-7 link is on the Coat or the Fuzz row, never both, and a path to another kind reads on the slot's own row (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). One precedence rule, `sets::slot_shown` (the one display reader; `v.slots` keeps its meaning): a link on the row, then an own or borrowed file whose game slot holds a link on another row or on none (`Replaced`), then an own or borrowed file under `slotN = ""` (`Cleared`), then own or borrowed, then the record, then empty. The row shows what the JSON carries.
  - **The row.** A link reads as filled: "{file}" and the link "from set {short}" that opens that set (AccessKit "Open set {short} ({slot label})", hover "Open the set this file comes from."), or "{file} · set by path"; the button **Unlink**; no format, alpha or channel-source controls and no status dot. Both sides stay inside the File cell: the file name is cut at the end, the link side takes at most half of the cell and is cut at its end, each with its full text on hover, and a linked row never makes the File column wider (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). On a shared game slot (3 or 7) a linked slot's row belongs to the link's kind, whether or not the set has an own file there, and shows **Unlink**, never Assign… (`sets::view_row`, which `editor_cells` and the status line's slot reasons build on). "Match on this" is not drawn on a linked slot 1 or 2; the Matching group keeps it. **Unlink** and **Use this file** are off on a pinned slot, with the slot menu's reason (`AppModel::slot_command_off`). A link to a slot that has no row (no shader pick and no own file there) draws no row (`// shortcut:` in `view_row`).
  - **Under a link.** An own file under a link for another kind (slot 7: an `_f` under a link to a `_cnr`) does not read as filled: the row shows the link and "{file} is not written: slot {n} is linked.", no chip is drawn for it and the chip count does not count it, and the 3D preview does not serve it (controller ruling 2, round 2: "when a link holds a game slot, an own or borrowed file of a different kind in that same game slot is not in the JSON, so it does not read as filled"; controller rulings, round 3: (1) "a Replaced slot draws no chip, the linked kind's chip carries the link"; (2) "when a game slot is linked its shared row belongs to the link's kind, and it shows Unlink"). A `slotN = ""` over an own or borrowed file reads "Left out of the JSON: you cleared this slot." with **Use this file**. The Assign menu keeps **Clear**. A link picked from the Assign menu logs one Info line to Messages ("{slot label} of {set short}: {file} (from set {from short})").
  - **The chip.** A linked chip is filled and named "{letter}, from set {short}" ("{letter}, path {file}" for a typed path), hover "{slot} linked to {path}", with the link mark painted in a 10 px square at the chip's top centre, between the two lock cells, in every lock state (controller ruling, 2026-11-18); the locks stay on a linked chip. A cleared slot's chip is the dim letter box "{letter}, cleared", hover "{slot} (cleared): {file}". An own file whose slot is linked (of any kind) or cleared draws no trailing thumbnail, and `chip_rows` counts what is drawn (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). The cleared chip and the typed-path box sense a click for the slot menu, so they are `Role::Button` and Tab stops (egui-ui-rules 8.5).
  - **The missing-map check.** A `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` warning for a slot that holds a link with a row is dropped (`link_slots`): the JSON names a file for it.
  - **Resolving.** `txst_map::link_slots` finds a link's source by the game path a link to it is written with (`game_path_in`), so the opened folder does not matter; the first set in display order that writes a path keeps it. The JSON's slot paths are unchanged (`slotN` already won); the feature keys follow JR8.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **JR9 A cleared slot turns no feature on.** Controller ruling 5, round 3: "an own file under `slotN = ""` turns no feature on, which changes exported JSON for such sets: the controller's call, for the owner to overrule". An own or borrowed file under `slotN = ""` is left out of the JSON, so `feature_slots` drops every kind of that game slot and the file no longer defaults its feature on (`emissive` for a cleared slot 3, and so on); a key the set writes itself stays. This holds only for a set that exports a JSON entry (`features` is computed where `exports_json` holds, as `linked` is); a set without one writes no `slotN`, so its clear changes nothing. A clear counts only where it removes an own or borrowed fill. For such JSON sets the other `feature_slots` readers follow: a cleared height takes a Landscape or PBR Landscape set out of CHK-PARALLAX-SCALE's comparison, and a cleared `_cnr` or `_f` blocks nothing. The exported JSON changes for a set whose cleared slot (3, 4, 7, 8) held a file that defaulted a feature on; no pinned test changed.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note JR, 2026-11-18)
- **JR7 The 3D preview serves a link (E1, 3D).** `set_preview::member_slots` serves a linked slot's file at the link's path, keyed by the row the link fills (`link.row`), and does not serve that row's own fill. A link with an item but no row (game slot 5 with no kind of the set on it) is not served (`// shortcut:`), and a typed path that names no set output pushes nothing, so the own fill keeps its prefix path. An own file under a link for another kind (`Replaced`) is not served (JR1). Links are read only for a set with a JSON entry. The member key is not `link.kind`, because `Encoder.members` is keyed by kind and a link to another slot's kind would evict the set's own fill of that kind (controller ruling, 2026-11-18).

  - **The row.** A link reads as filled: "{file}" and the link "from set {short}" that opens that set (AccessKit "Open set {short} ({slot label})", hover "Open the set this file comes from."), or "{file} · set by path"; the button **Unlink**; no format, alpha or channel-source controls and no status dot. "Match on this" is not drawn on a linked slot 1 or 2; the Matching group keeps it. **Unlink** and **Use this file** are off on a pinned slot, with the slot menu's reason (`AppModel::slot_command_off`). A link to a slot that has no row (no shader pick and no own file there) draws no row (`// shortcut:` in `view_row`).
  - **Under a link.** An own file under a link for another kind (slot 7: an `_f` under a link to a `_cnr`) does not read as filled: the row shows the link and "{file} is not written: slot {n} is linked.", no chip is drawn for it and the chip count does not count it, and the 3D preview does not serve it (controller ruling 2, round 2: "when a link holds a game slot, an own or borrowed file of a different kind in that same game slot is not in the JSON, so it does not read as filled"; controller rulings, round 3: (1) "a Replaced slot draws no chip, the linked kind's chip carries the link"; (2) "when a game slot is linked its shared row belongs to the link's kind, and it shows Unlink"). On a set that writes an entry, a `slotN = ""` over an own or borrowed file reads "Left out of the JSON: you cleared this slot." with **Use this file**; on any other set no JSON carries the clear, and the file shows as itself (JR14). The Assign menu keeps **Clear**. A link picked from the Assign menu logs one Info line to Messages ("{slot label} of {set short}: {file} (from set {from short})").

A cleared slot's chip is the dim letter box "{letter}, cleared", hover "{slot} (cleared): {file}".
